# Technical Reference: observer, sweep, living work report and Orca dashboard design (IronClaude)

## Task Overview

This task produces one Technical Reference document, written to `/home/coder/workspaces/IronClaude/.dev/brainstorms/20261120-pr686-unify/techref/OBSERVER-SWEEP-DASHBOARD-TECHNICAL-REFERENCE.md` (NOT under `docs/`), for the "observer, sweep and dashboard" family of the IronClaude repository: (a) the observer `superclaude observe` in `src/superclaude/cli/observe/`; (b) the sweep `superclaude sweep run`, the command `/sc:sweep` and the skill `sc-sweep-protocol`, in `src/superclaude/cli/sweep/` and the coordinator contract `src/superclaude/core/WORKER-CONTRACT.md` (duty WC-16); (c) the living work report skill `src/superclaude/skills/agent-work-report/`; (d) the Orca dashboard / TUI design package of issue 409 under `/home/coder/workspaces/IronClaude/.dev/design/orca-observer-20261106/`.

The owner needs an exact, cited picture of what exists, to decide in a later design brainstorm how Clay's new "orchestrator" design (a per-repo orchestrator woken by the issue watcher, plus a living work report) should be unified with the owner's observer, sweep and planned TUI dashboard. The reference therefore emphasises: data sources, output formats, what is live, who consumes the output, and overlaps in purpose, plus how each relates to the coordinator (WORKER-CONTRACT.md sweep duty WC-16), what data each reads from Orca, what each writes, and what is built versus only designed. It states facts and overlaps and does NOT recommend a unification.

The work runs the tech-reference skill pipeline in seven phases: preparation; six parallel read-only code/design investigation agents (R1-R6); a standard-intensity research completeness gate; skipped web research; seven synthesis agents plus a standard-intensity synthesis gate; single-agent assembly (rf-assembler) plus a seven-agent lens QA gate and a two-agent source-fidelity gate; and task completion. Scope discovery is already done (see `research-notes.md` and `scope/`); research agents run when this task file is executed. All code is read from the read-only snapshot of origin/master 61ac2c2e6 at `/home/coder/.cache/ic-snap-20261120/master`. Nothing is staged or committed.

## Key Objectives

The following objectives MUST be achieved by this task:

1. **Evidence-cited reference:** One assembled Technical Reference at the OUTPUT_PATH above, Standard tier (800-1,200 lines target; the assembler may run to the upper end because four subjects are covered), following all 16 sections of `technical_reference_template.md` (sections 6 and 7 marked N/A with rationale unless the agents find a clear state model or component inventory), every claim cited with `file:line`, and doc-sourced claims tagged [CODE-VERIFIED] / [CODE-CONTRADICTED] / [UNVERIFIED].
2. **Decision-relevant parts present and uncut:** the "family at a glance" comparison matrix in Section 2 (data sources x outputs x live/designed x consumers x overlaps, plus the relationship diagram with the coordinator and WC-16), the Orca verb inventory and schemas in Section 8, and the contradictions / UNVERIFIED / DEFECT-CANDIDATE list in Section 14.
3. **C8 rule honoured:** the sweep classes `stalled (proved)` and `fenced` (declared in the command, skill and WC-16 but unable to fire in Phase 1 because nothing sets `worker_done_seen` / `fenced_evidence`) and any other in-scope unwired capability are recorded as DEFECT or DEFECT-CANDIDATE with the declaring spec/design line, the code evidence and the cited on-record scope decision if one exists; absent such a decision the reference states plainly "remediation task not created (read-only brief); flagged for the owner". Never relabelled as a plain known gap without the cited decision.
4. **All quality gates passed:** Gate 1 research completeness (3 agents), Gate 2 synthesis quality (3 lenses, each partitioned across 2 instances), Gate 3 final-document lens QA (7 agents), Gate 4 source fidelity (2 agents); serialized fix protocol, max 2 fix cycles (3 reviews) per gate, then HALT and escalate.
5. **Read-only discipline:** no source or design file is modified; only files inside the task folder and the single OUTPUT_PATH file are written; nothing is staged or committed; the final return to the requester is only the final path and a three-line summary.

## Detailed Task Instructions

**CRITICAL:** Follow the tech-reference skill workflow (`/home/coder/workspaces/IronClaude/.claude/skills/tech-reference/SKILL.md`) as encoded in the phases below. Every checklist item is a self-contained prompt (one paragraph). Agent items embed the full agent prompt, which always begins with the GLOBAL AGENT RULES block from the requester. Items that spawn independent agents inside the same phase may be spawned in parallel (one message, multiple Agent tool calls) per the F2a parallel-spawning exception; mark each item individually as its agent returns.

**Review-loop contract used by every QA gate below:** blocking = a real defect (wrong behaviour, a false claim, a missing required deliverable, a security or data-loss risk); minor = cosmetic, wording, formatting, citation format, a nit, or a moved line number on a true claim. Minor findings are recorded as notes, never fail a review, are never rechecked and never trigger a fix pass. A gate runs at most 2 fix cycles (3 reviews); after that HALT and escalate to the requester with the open blocking findings. Halt-precedence per cycle transition: (1) regression check first (if any item that passed in the previous cycle now fails, HALT with `Regression detected on Item X.Y, previously PASS at cycle N, now FAIL. Halt overrides monotonicity check.`), (2) then monotonicity (if the number of blocking findings is not lower than in the previous cycle, HALT with `[HALT-MONOTONICITY] |F|=<n>`), (3) then the cap, (4) then proceed. Each gate keeps its own independent counters.

## Open Questions (builder notes for the skill)

- **Research-agent input sizes vs SKILL.md Critical Rule 17 (about 1,000 lines per agent):** the assignments R1-R6 come from the requester's research notes and each reads well over 1,000 lines (for example R4 reads `sweep/probes.py` at 1,477 lines plus `report.py` and `commands.py`; R1 reads `parse.py` at 835 lines plus `adapter.py`, `collector.py`). The task file keeps the requester's six-agent plan (BUILD-REQUEST.md Phase 2 and research-notes SUGGESTED_PHASES override); depth is protected by the incremental-writing protocol, the research-depth lens and the gap-filling cycle in Gate 1. The skill may want to split R1 and R4 if Gate 1 reports shallow coverage.
- **Gate 4 lens assignment:** BUILD-REQUEST.md fixes 2 fidelity agents with the lenses semantic-coverage and detail-preservation and ALSO disjoint source halves (observer + sweep; agent-work-report + design package). The task file gives each agent both lenses on its own half so that every source file is checked under both lenses; a one-lens-per-agent reading would leave each half checked under only one lens.
- **Fidelity source size:** the source halves total far more than 1,000 lines (the requester notes this and fixes the count at 2 for standard intensity); fidelity agents are told to work module by module and write findings incrementally.
- **Dynamic content and task_type:** the Gate 1 gap-filling items are dynamic (one agent per blocking gap, unknown at build time), so the frontmatter has `task_type: dynamic` and the two gap-fill sections use DYNAMIC CONTENT markers with a conditional template item. All other items are enumerated statically. Cycle-2 re-runs of Gates 2 to 4 are encoded as short conditional items that re-read the cycle-1 item of this file and substitute file names (the F1 loop re-reads the task file every item, so the prompts are not lost across sessions).
- **reflect_pre:** left at the template default (not run for this build); `reflect_post_mode: skill` is set as requested and no POST reflect item is emitted (documentation-only task, no spec resolves).
- **C8 versus the read-only brief:** the requester forbids writing outside the task folder and the output file, so the C8 clause's "open or link an MDTM remediation task" cannot be done; every research, synthesis, QA and assembler prompt carries the requester's override (record DEFECT-CANDIDATE with the declaring line, code evidence and cited scope decision; if none, state "remediation task not created (read-only brief); flagged for the owner").
- **Facts outside the repo stay UNVERIFIED:** Orca automations, `.dev/coordinator/` contents and the `orchestrator_repo` key in `issue-watch.json` cannot be read from the snapshot; the prompts tell agents to mark them UNVERIFIED rather than assume.
